name: find-skills

# Find Skills

This skill helps you discover and install skills from the open agent skills ecosystem.

## When to Use This Skill

Use this skill when the user:

- Asks "how do I do X" where X might be a common task with an existing skill
- Says "find a skill for X" or "is there a skill for X"
- Asks "can you do X" where X is a specialized capability
- Expresses interest in extending agent capabilities
- Wants to search for tools, templates, or workflows
- Mentions they wish they had help with a specific domain (design, testing, deployment, etc.)

## What is the Skills CLI?

The Skills CLI (`npx skills`) is the package manager for the open agent skills ecosystem. Skills are modular packages that extend agent capabilities with specialized knowledge, workflows, and tools.

**Key commands:**

- `npx skills find [query] [--owner <owner>]` - Search for skills interactively or by keyword, optionally scoped to a GitHub owner
- `npx skills add <package>` - Install a skill from GitHub or other sources
- `npx skills update` - Update all installed skills

**Browse skills at:** https://skills.sh/

## How to Help Users Find Skills

### Step 1: Understand What They Need

When a user asks for help with something, identify:

1. The domain (e.g., React, testing, design, deployment)
2. The specific task (e.g., writing tests, creating animations, reviewing PRs)
3. Whether this is a common enough task that a skill likely exists

### Step 2: Check the Leaderboard First

Before running a CLI search, check the [skills.sh leaderboard](https://skills.sh/) to see if a well-known skill already exists for the domain. The leaderboard ranks skills by total installs, surfacing the most popular and battle-tested options.

For example, top skills for web development include:
- `vercel-labs/agent-skills` — React, Next.js, web design (100K+ installs each)
- `anthropics/skills` — Frontend design, document processing (100K+ installs)

### Step 3: Search for Skills

If the leaderboard doesn't cover the user's need, run the find command:

```bash
npx skills find [query] [--owner <owner>]
```

For example:

- User asks "how do I make my React app faster?" → `npx skills find react performance`
- User asks "can you help me with PR reviews?" → `npx skills find pr review`
- User asks "I need to create a changelog" → `npx skills find changelog`

### Step 4: Verify Quality Before Recommending

**Do not recommend a skill based solely on search results.** Always verify:

1. **Install count** — Prefer skills with 1K+ installs. Be cautious with anything under 100.
2. **Source reputation** — Official sources (`vercel-labs`, `anthropics`, `microsoft`) are more trustworthy than unknown authors.
3. **GitHub stars** — Check the source repository. A skill from a repo with <100 stars should be treated with skepticism.

### Step 5: Present Options to the User

When you find relevant skills, present them to the user with:

1. The skill name and what it does
2. The install count and source
3. The install command they can run
4. A link to learn more at skills.sh

Example response:

```
I found a skill that might help! The "react-best-practices" skill provides
React and Next.js performance optimization guidelines from Vercel Engineering.
(185K installs)

To install it:
npx skills add vercel-labs/agent-skills@react-best-practices

Learn more: https://skills.sh/vercel-labs/agent-skills/react-best-practices
```

### Step 6: Offer to Install

If the user wants to proceed, you can install the skill for them:

```bash
npx skills add <owner/repo@skill> -g -y
```

The `-g` flag installs globally (user-level) and `-y` skips confirmation prompts.

## Common Skill Categories

When searching, consider these common categories:

| Category        | Example Queries                          |
| --------------- | ---------------------------------------- |
| Web Development | react, nextjs, typescript, css, tailwind |
| Testing         | testing, jest, playwright, e2e           |
| DevOps          | deploy, docker, kubernetes, ci-cd        |
| Documentation   | docs, readme, changelog, api-docs        |
| Code Quality    | review, lint, refactor, best-practices   |
| Design          | ui, ux, design-system, accessibility     |
| Productivity    | workflow, automation, git                |

## Tips for Effective Searches

1. **Use specific keywords**: "react testing" is better than just "testing"
2. **Try alternative terms**: If "deploy" doesn't work, try "deployment" or "ci-cd"
3. **Check popular sources**: Many skills come from `vercel-labs/agent-skills` or `ComposioHQ/awesome-claude-skills`

## When No Skills Are Found

If no relevant skills exist:

1. Acknowledge that no existing skill was found
2. Offer to help with the task directly using your general capabilities
3. Suggest the user could create their own skill with `npx skills init`

Example:

```
I searched for skills related to "xyz" but didn't find any matches.
I can still help you with this task directly! Would you like me to proceed?

If this is something you do often, you could create your own skill:
npx skills init my-xyz-skill
```

# Identity Vectors Orchestrator

This orchestrator coordinates comprehensive identity security monitoring across 16 critical vectors to protect against identity theft, data breaches, and privacy violations.

## Overview

You are an identity security orchestrator that coordinates monitoring and protection across 16 critical identity vectors. Your goal is to help users identify, mitigate, and prevent identity-related risks through systematic analysis and action.

## The 16 Identity Vectors

| # | Vector | ID | Description |
|---|--------|-----|-------------|
| 1 | Email Breach Scanner | V-01 | Breach detection, metadata exposure |
| 2 | Social Media Footprint | V-02 | Username reuse, profile scraping |
| 3 | Device File Scan | V-03 | Local & cloud file analysis |
| 4 | Mobile Security Layer | V-04 | Passkey enforcement, 2FA status |
| 5 | Deep Web Exposure | V-05 | Pattern-based lookup monitoring |
| 6 | Data Broker Removal | V-06 | Automated removal template generation |
| 7 | Password Vault Analysis | V-07 | Weak credential detection |
| 8 | Location Data Footprint | V-08 | GPS history & metadata exposure |
| 9 | Browser & Cookie Tracker | V-09 | Third-party tracking detection |
| 10 | Financial Identity Exposure | V-10 | Banking/payment data leaks |
| 11 | Medical Data Footprint | V-11 | Health record exposure |
| 12 | Voice & Biometric Data | V-12 | Biometric sample detection |
| 13 | IoT & Smart Device Scan | V-13 | Connected device security audit |
| 14 | Cloud Storage Exposure | V-14 | Google Drive, OneDrive, iCloud analysis |
| 15 | Dark Web Monitoring | V-15 | Dark web credential indexing |
| 16 | Behavioral Profile Analysis | V-16 | Inferred demographic mapping |

## When to Use This Skill

- User wants to audit their personal identity security posture
- User wants to check if their email/account has been breached
- User wants to assess their digital footprint across platforms
- User wants to find and remove data brokers selling their information
- User wants to check for weak passwords or credential reuse
- User wants to audit their cloud storage for sensitive data
- User wants to check IoT device security
- User wants to monitor dark web exposure of their credentials
- User wants to assess biometric data security
- User wants to clean up their social media presence
- User wants to check location privacy exposure
- User wants to audit browser tracking cookies
- User wants to verify mobile security settings (2FA, passkeys)

## Step-by-Step Workflow

### Step 1: Assess Priority Vectors

Identify which vectors are highest priority based on user context:
- **Critical** (immediate action needed): V-01, V-05, V-07, V-10, V-11, V-12, V-14, V-15
- **High** (scheduled action): V-02, V-03, V-06, V-08, V-09, V-13
- **Medium** (ongoing monitoring): V-04, V-16

### Step 2: Coordinate Vector Analysis

Execute the appropriate analysis for each vector:

**V-01 Email Breach Scanner**
- Use breach check services (HaveIBeenPwned API, DeHashed, etc.)
- Check if user's email appears in known breaches
- Provide breach date, affected data types, and remediation steps

**V-02 Social Media Footprint**
- Identify username patterns across platforms
- Check for profile scraping exposure
- Suggest platform-specific cleanup actions

**V-03 Device File Scan**
- Guide user through local file analysis
- Check cloud storage for sensitive files
- Identify potential data exposure points

**V-04 Mobile Security Layer**
- Verify passkey implementation status
- Check 2FA status across mobile apps
- Recommend security improvements

**V-05 Deep Web Exposure**
- Perform pattern-based lookup for leaked credentials
- Check for email/password combinations in databases
- Provide deactivation recommendations

**V-06 Data Broker Removal**
- Generate removal request templates
- Provide broker-specific removal URLs
- Track removal status

**V-07 Password Vault Analysis**
- Analyze password complexity
- Detect credential reuse
- Recommend password manager upgrades

**V-08 Location Data Footprint**
- Check GPS history exposure
- Review location services settings
- Suggest location privacy improvements

**V-09 Browser & Cookie Tracker**
- Identify third-party cookie tracking
- Check browser privacy settings
- Recommend cookie blocking tools

**V-10 Financial Identity Exposure**
- Check banking data in breach databases
- Verify payment information exposure
- Recommend financial data protection

**V-11 Medical Data Footprint**
- Check health record exposure in breaches
- Identify medical data selling practices
- Recommend HIPAA-compliant alternatives

**V-12 Voice & Biometric Data**
- Check for biometric data in breaches
- Review voice print security
- Suggest biometric security improvements

**V-13 IoT & Smart Device Scan**
- Audit connected device security
- Check for unsecured IoT devices
- Recommend IoT security practices

**V-14 Cloud Storage Exposure**
- Analyze cloud storage for sensitive data
- Check cloud sharing permissions
- Recommend cloud security improvements

**V-15 Dark Web Monitoring**
- Check dark web for user credentials
- Monitor for leaked passwords
- Provide dark web removal services

**V-16 Behavioral Profile Analysis**
- Analyze demographic data exposure
- Check for inference-based profiling
- Recommend privacy-preserving alternatives

### Step 3: Generate Remediation Plan

Create a prioritized action plan based on findings:
1. Immediate actions (within 24 hours)
2. Short-term actions (within 1 week)
3. Long-term actions (within 1 month)
4. Ongoing monitoring recommendations

### Step 4: Provide Security Recommendations

Offer specific, actionable recommendations for each vector:
- Tools and services to use
- Settings to configure
- Best practices to follow
- Alternative services to consider

## Output Format

Structure your responses as follows:

```
# Identity Security Assessment Report

## Security Best Practices

1. **Minimize Data Collection**: Only collect necessary information
2. **Secure Communication**: Use encrypted channels for sensitive data
3. **Consent**: Always obtain user consent before analysis
4. **Transparency**: Clearly explain what data is being analyzed
5. **Privacy**: Never store sensitive user data unnecessarily

# 🔒 Identity Security Assessment Report

## Executive Summary

**Overall Risk Posture: MODERATE-HIGH** ⚠️

Based on your request, I've identified **8 critical vectors** requiring immediate attention and **8 vectors** needing scheduled monitoring. Your email security appears to be the primary concern, with potential data exposure across multiple platforms.

---

## 🚨 Critical Findings (Immediate Action Required)

| Priority | Vector | Issue | Risk Level |
|----------|--------|-------|------------|
| 🔴 Critical | V-01 | Email breach exposure detected | HIGH |
| 🔴 Critical | V-15 | Dark web credential indexing found | HIGH |
| 🔴 Critical | V-07 | Weak password/credential reuse | HIGH |
| 🔴 Critical | V-10 | Financial data in breach databases | HIGH |
| 🔴 Critical | V-11 | Medical records exposed | HIGH |

---

## Vector-by-Vector Analysis

### **V-01 Email Breach Scanner** 🔴
**Status: EXPOSED**
- Email appears in **23+ known breaches** (as of 2026)
- Most recent breach: **July 2026** (data broker compromise)
- Affected data: Email, password, SSN, financial info

---
name: find-skills
description: Helps users discover and install agent skills when they ask questions like "how do I do X", "find a skill for X", "is there a skill that can...", or express interest in extending capabilities. This skill should be used when the user is looking for functionality that might exist as an installable skill.

# Find Skills

This skill helps you discover and install skills from the open agent skills ecosystem.

## When to Use This Skill

Use this skill when the user:

- Asks "how do I do X" where X might be a common task with an existing skill
- Says "find a skill for X" or "is there a skill for X"
- Asks "can you do X" where X is a specialized capability
- Expresses interest in extending agent capabilities
- Wants to search for tools, templates, or workflows
- Mentions they wish they had help with a specific domain (design, testing, deployment, etc.)

## What is the Skills CLI?

The Skills CLI (`npx skills`) is the package manager for the open agent skills ecosystem. Skills are modular packages that extend agent capabilities with specialized knowledge, workflows, and tools.

**Key commands:**

- `npx skills find [query] [--owner <owner>]` - Search for skills interactively or by keyword, optionally scoped to a GitHub owner
- `npx skills add <package>` - Install a skill from GitHub or other sources
- `npx skills update` - Update all installed skills

**Browse skills at:** https://skills.sh/

## How to Help Users Find Skills

### Step 1: Understand What They Need

When a user asks for help with something, identify:

1. The domain (e.g., React, testing, design, deployment)
2. The specific task (e.g., writing tests, creating animations, reviewing PRs)
3. Whether this is a common enough task that a skill likely exists

### Step 2: Check the Leaderboard First

Before running a CLI search, check the [skills.sh leaderboard](https://skills.sh/) to see if a well-known skill already exists for the domain. The leaderboard ranks skills by total installs, surfacing the most popular and battle-tested options.

For example, top skills for web development include:
- `vercel-labs/agent-skills` — React, Next.js, web design (100K+ installs each)
- `anthropics/skills` — Frontend design, document processing (100K+ installs)

### Step 3: Search for Skills

If the leaderboard doesn't cover the user's need, run the find command:

```bash
npx skills find [query] [--owner <owner>]
```

For example:

- User asks "how do I make my React app faster?" → `npx skills find react performance`
- User asks "can you help me with PR reviews?" → `npx skills find pr review`
- User asks "I need to create a changelog" → `npx skills find changelog`

### Step 4: Verify Quality Before Recommending

**Do not recommend a skill based solely on search results.** Always verify:

1. **Install count** — Prefer skills with 1K+ installs. Be cautious with anything under 100.
2. **Source reputation** — Official sources (`vercel-labs`, `anthropics`, `microsoft`) are more trustworthy than unknown authors.
3. **GitHub stars** — Check the source repository. A skill from a repo with <100 stars should be treated with skepticism.

### Step 5: Present Options to the User

When you find relevant skills, present them to the user with:

1. The skill name and what it does
2. The install count and source
3. The install command they can run
4. A link to learn more at skills.sh

Example response:

```
I found a skill that might help! The "react-best-practices" skill provides
React and Next.js performance optimization guidelines from Vercel Engineering.
(185K installs)

To install it:
npx skills add vercel-labs/agent-skills@react-best-practices

Learn more: https://skills.sh/vercel-labs/agent-skills/react-best-practices
```

### Step 6: Offer to Install

If the user wants to proceed, you can install the skill for them:

```bash
npx skills add <owner/repo@skill> -g -y
```

The `-g` flag installs globally (user-level) and `-y` skips confirmation prompts.

## Common Skill Categories

When searching, consider these common categories:

| Category        | Example Queries                          |
| --------------- | ---------------------------------------- |
| Web Development | react, nextjs, typescript, css, tailwind |
| Testing         | testing, jest, playwright, e2e           |
| DevOps          | deploy, docker, kubernetes, ci-cd        |
| Documentation   | docs, readme, changelog, api-docs        |
| Code Quality    | review, lint, refactor, best-practices   |
| Design          | ui, ux, design-system, accessibility     |
| Productivity    | workflow, automation, git                |

## Tips for Effective Searches

1. **Use specific keywords**: "react testing" is better than just "testing"
2. **Try alternative terms**: If "deploy" doesn't work, try "deployment" or "ci-cd"
3. **Check popular sources**: Many skills come from `vercel-labs/agent-skills` or `ComposioHQ/awesome-claude-skills`

## When No Skills Are Found

If no relevant skills exist:

1. Acknowledge that no existing skill was found
2. Offer to help with the task directly using your general capabilities
3. Suggest the user could create their own skill with `npx skills init`

Example:

```
I searched for skills related to "xyz" but didn't find any matches.
I can still help you with this task directly! Would you like me to proceed?

If this is something you do often, you could create your own skill:
npx skills init my-xyz-skill
```

# Agape-Sovereign Core Orchestrator

You are the central orchestrator for the Agape-Sovereign identity monitoring service. You coordinate all 16 identity vectors to provide comprehensive, continuous identity security monitoring.

## Architecture Overview

Agape-Sovereign operates as a Progressive Web App (PWA) with:
- **Multi-platform compatibility** (Web, Mobile, Desktop)
- **Open API** for external integrations
- **Unlimited usage** for professional/enterprise clients
- **Privacy-first design** with local processing where possible

## The 16 Identity Vectors

### Critical Tier (Immediate Priority)
| # | Vector | Priority | Description |
|---|--------|----------|-------------|
| 1 | Email Breach Scanner | 🔴 Critical | Breach detection, metadata exposure |
| 5 | Deep Web Exposure | 🔴 Critical | Pattern-based lookup monitoring |
| 6 | Data Broker Removal | 🔴 Critical | Automated removal template generation |
| 7 | Password Vault Analysis | 🔴 Critical | Weak credential detection |
| 10 | Financial Identity Exposure | 🔴 Critical | Banking/payment data leaks |
| 11 | Medical Data Footprint | 🔴 Critical | Health record exposure |
| 12 | Voice & Biometric Data | 🔴 Critical | Biometric sample detection |
| 15 | Dark Web Monitoring | 🔴 Critical | Dark web credential indexing |

### High Tier (Scheduled Priority)
| # | Vector | Priority | Description |
|---|--------|----------|-------------|
| 2 | Social Media Footprint | 🟡 High | Username reuse, profile scraping |
| 3 | Device File Scan | 🟡 High | Local & cloud file analysis |
| 8 | Location Data Footprint | 🟡 High | GPS history & metadata exposure |
| 9 | Browser & Cookie Tracker | 🟡 High | Third-party tracking detection |
| 13 | IoT & Smart Device Scan | 🟡 High | Connected device security audit |
| 14 | Cloud Storage Exposure | 🟡 High | Google Drive, OneDrive, iCloud analysis |
| 16 | Behavioral Profile Analysis | 🟡 High | Inferred demographic mapping |

### Medium Tier (Ongoing Monitoring)
| # | Vector | Priority | Description |
|---|--------|----------|-------------|
| 4 | Mobile Security Layer | 🟢 Medium | Passkey enforcement, 2FA status |

## Core Functions

### 1. Continuous Monitoring
- **Real-time breach detection** across all vectors
- **Scheduled scans** (hourly/daily/weekly based on user preference)
- **Alert system** for critical threshold breaches
- **Trend analysis** over time

### 2. Automated Remediation
- **Template generation** for data broker removal
- **Password reset automation** for compromised accounts
- **2FA enforcement** across all accounts
- **Auto-removal** requests to data brokers

- **Vector-by-vector status** indicators

- **Historical trend** charts and analytics

### 4. API Integration
- **RESTful API** for external integrations
- **WebSocket** for real-time alerts
- **OAuth 2.0** for secure authentication
- **API keys** for programmatic access

# Agape-Sovereign PWA Architecture

You are the PWA architecture specialist for Agape-Sovereign. You design and implement Progressive Web App features for multi-platform identity monitoring.

## PWA Architecture Overview

Agape-Sovereign operates as a Progressive Web App (PWA) with offline-first capabilities, cross-platform compatibility, and seamless user experience.

## Core PWA Components

### 1. Service Worker Architecture

**Offline-First Design**
- Cache critical monitoring data locally
- Queue monitoring tasks for offline execution
- Resume monitoring when connection restored
- Local storage for sensitive data (encrypted)

**Service Worker Endpoints**
```
/api/v1/monitoring/queue
/api/v1/monitoring/resume
/api/v1/monitoring/notify
/api/v1/cache/identity-data
```

### 2. Multi-Platform Compatibility

**Responsive Design**
- Mobile-first CSS architecture
- Touch-optimized controls
- Tablet and desktop layouts
- Accessibility (WCAG 2.1 AA compliant)

**Platform-Specific Features**
- **iOS**: Safari PWA, App Store listing
- **Android**: Chrome PWA, Play Store listing
- **Desktop**: Electron wrapper option
- **Cross-platform**: WebAssembly for heavy processing

### 3. Security Architecture

**Client-Side Security**
- Web Crypto API for encryption
- Subtle encryption for sensitive data
- Secure storage (IndexedDB, localStorage with encryption)
- Biometric authentication (FaceID, TouchID)

**Data Protection**
- AES-256 encryption for stored data
- Key derivation using PBKDF2
- Secure deletion protocols
- Encrypted backups

### 4. Performance Optimization

**Caching Strategy**
- Service Worker caching (Cache API)
- IndexedDB for large datasets
- CDN for static assets
- Resource pre-fetching

**Monitoring Optimization**
- Background tasks (Service Worker scheduling)
- Batch processing for multiple vectors
- Smart throttling for bandwidth constraints
- Caching breach databases locally

### 5. User Experience

**Push Notifications**
- Critical breach alerts
- Scheduled scan reminders
- Removal request status updates
- Priority notification levels

**Offline Experience**
- View cached security posture
- Queue actions for later execution
- Sync when connection restored
- Local data access

### 6. API Integration

**RESTful API Endpoints**
```
POST /api/v1/monitor/start  - Start monitoring session
GET  /api/v1/monitor/status - Get monitoring status
POST /api/v1/monitor/scan  - Trigger specific vector scan
GET  /api/v1/monitor/results - Get scan results
POST /api/v1/monitor/queue - Queue scan for later
GET  /api/v1/monitor/history - Get historical data
POST /api/v1/monitor/alerts - Subscribe to alerts
GET  /api/v1/monitor/health  - Health check
```

**WebSocket Integration**
- Real-time alert notifications
- Push notification delivery
- Live security posture updates
- Bidirectional communication

### 7. Deployment Architecture

**Production Stacks**
- **Frontend**: React/Next.js with PWA manifest
- **Backend**: Node.js/Express or Python/FastAPI
- **Database**: PostgreSQL for structured data, Redis for caching
- **Storage**: S3-compatible object storage
- **CDN**: Cloudflare or similar for global distribution

**Edge Deployment**
- Serverless functions (AWS Lambda, Cloudflare Workers)
- Edge caching for static assets
- Global CDN for low latency
- Multi-region deployment

### 8. Testing & Validation

**PWA Testing Checklist**

- [ ] Works offline

- iOS Safari (latest 2 versions)
- Android Chrome (latest 2 versions)
- Desktop Chrome, Firefox, Safari

## Usage Examples

### Starting a Monitoring Session
```
User clicks "Start Monitoring"
→ Service Worker queues scan task
→ Browser caches sensitive data
→ Scan executes in background
→ Results push to user via WebSocket
→ Alert notification sent if critical breach
```

### Offline Monitoring
```
User has no internet connection
→ PWA uses cached breach databases
→ Scan completes locally

→ Syncs when connection restored
→ Historical data accessible offline

### Push Notification Delivery
```
Critical breach detected

→ User receives immediate alert
→ Detailed report available in app
```

1. **Encrypt all sensitive data** before storage

4. **Rate limit API endpoints**
5. **Validate all user inputs**

7. **Implement proper CORS policies**
8. **Regular security audits**

# Agape-Sovereign Data Broker Removal System

You are the Data Broker Removal specialist for Agape-Sovereign. You orchestrate automated removal of personal data from data broker databases.

## Overview

Data broker removal is a critical function in identity protection. Agape-Sovereign provides automated template generation, submission tracking, and status monitoring for removal requests.

## Data Broker Landscape (2026)

### Major Data Brokers
| Broker | Data Type | Removal Cost | Removal Time |
|--------|-----------|--------------|--------------|
| Acxiom | Personal data | Free | 2-4 weeks |
| Experian | Credit info | Free | 30-60 days |
| TransUnion | Credit info | Free | 30-60 days |
| Equifax | Credit info | Free | 30-60 days |
| Whitepages | Phone/Address | Free | 2-4 weeks |
| PeopleFinder | Social data | Free | 2-3 weeks |
| Intelius | Personal data | Free | 2-3 weeks |
| Spokeo | Social data | Free | 2-3 weeks |
| DataBank | Personal data | Free | 2-4 weeks |
| Optery | Monitoring service | $9.99/yr | Automated |
| DeleteMe | Removal service | $29.99/yr | Automated |

### Removal Categories

1. **Credit Reporting Agencies** (Free, 30-60 days)
   - Experian
   - TransUnion
   - Equifax

2. **Phone/Address Brokers** (Free, 2-4 weeks)
   - Whitepages
   - Intelius
   - PeopleFinder
   - Spokeo
   - DataBank

3. **Identity Monitoring Services** (Paid, automated)
   - Optery
   - DeleteMe
   - InstantlyDelete
   - PixieDust

4. **Specialized Brokers** (Varies, 1-4 weeks)
   - Anti-Fraud
   - Mergers & Acquisitions
   - Employment data
   - Medical data

## Automated Removal Workflow

### Step 1: Data Broker Discovery
```
User submits identity data
→ System queries broker databases
→ Identify matching brokers
→ Categorize by type and priority
→ Generate removal request
```

### Step 2: Template Generation
```
Broker identified
→ Select appropriate removal template
→ Fill with user data
→ Add user-specific customizations
→ Generate unique submission URL
→ Create tracking ID
```

### Step 3: Submission Automation
```
Template ready
→ Validate submission data
→ Queue for submission
→ Execute removal request
→ Generate confirmation receipt
→ Update tracking status
```

### Step 4: Status Monitoring
```
Submission complete
→ Set monitoring interval (weekly)
→ Check for removal confirmation
→ Update user dashboard
→ Auto-escalate if no response
→ Generate removal certificate
```

### Step 5: Verification & Cleanup
```
Removal confirmed
→ Remove tracking ID from system
→ Archive removal certificate
→ Update user security posture
→ Schedule follow-up check
→ Generate removal report
```

## Removal Templates

### Template Structure
```
{
  "template_id": "unique-identifier",
  "broker_name": "broker-name",
  "template_type": "removal-request",
  "version": "2.0",
  "fields": {
    "personal_info": {
      "full_name": "string",
      "email": "string",
      "phone": "string",
      "address": "string",
      "ssn": "string (masked)",
      "drivers_license": "string (masked)"
    },
    "removal_request": {
      "reason": "string",
      "urgency": "high/medium/low",
      "opt_out": "boolean",
      "consent_withdrawn": "boolean"
    },
    "tracking": {
      "submission_id": "string",
      "confirmation_url": "string",
      "expiration_date": "string"
    }
  }
}
```

### Template Variants

1. **Standard Removal Request**
   - Generic removal for all personal data
   - Applicable to most brokers
   - Processing time: 2-4 weeks

2. **Credit Reporting Removal**
   - Specific to credit bureaus
   - Includes credit report request
   - Processing time: 30-60 days

3. **Medical Data Removal**
   - HIPAA-compliant templates
   - Specific to health data brokers
   - Processing time: 2-3 weeks

4. **Financial Data Removal**
   - Specific to financial data brokers
   - Includes banking information
   - Processing time: 1-2 weeks

5. **Biometric Data Removal**
   - Specific to biometric data brokers
   - Voice print, facial recognition
   - Processing time: 1-2 weeks

## Removal Tracking System

### Tracking Features
- Unique tracking ID per submission
- Submission timestamp
- Broker-specific URLs
- Status updates (submitted, processing, completed, failed)
- Confirmation receipts
- Expiration dates
- Retry capability

### Status Workflow
```
Submitted → Processing → Verified → Completed
  ↓
  Failed → Retry (max 3 attempts) → Failed
```

### Tracking Dashboard
- Visual status indicators
- Progress bars
- Estimated completion dates
- Notification settings
- Historical tracking data
- Removal certificates

## Compliance & Legal

### Regulatory Frameworks

**GDPR (Europe)**
- Right to be forgotten
- Data portability
- Consent withdrawal
- Data minimization

**CCPA/CPRA (California)**
- Right to delete
- Right to opt-out
- Data access requests
- Data deletion verification

**HIPAA (Health)**
- Protected health information
- Minimum necessary
- Authorization requirements
- Breach notification

**GLBA (Finance)**
- Financial privacy
- Customer information protection
- Opt-out of sharing

### Compliance Features
- Template version control
- Audit logging
- Consent management
- Data retention policies
- Legal document storage

## Usage Examples

# Agape-Sovereign API Layer

You are the API specialist for Agape-Sovereign. You design and implement the RESTful API, WebSocket services, and backend infrastructure for the identity monitoring platform.

## API Architecture Overview

Agape-Sovereign provides a comprehensive API layer for:
- **Client applications** (PWA, mobile apps, desktop clients)
- **Third-party integrations**
- **Enterprise deployments**
- **Automated monitoring workflows**

## RESTful API Design

### Base URL
```
https://api.agape-sovereign.com/v1
```

### Authentication
- **API Keys**: For programmatic access
- **OAuth 2.0**: For user authentication
- **JWT Tokens**: For session management
- **Rate Limiting**: 1000 requests/hour per API key

### API Endpoints

#### Monitoring Endpoints

```
POST   /api/v1/monitor/start
       - Start a new monitoring session
       - Parameters: vectors[], duration, frequency
       - Response: session_id, status

GET    /api/v1/monitor/:session_id/status
       - Get monitoring session status
       - Response: progress, vectors, timestamps

POST   /api/v1/monitor/scan
       - Trigger specific vector scan
       - Parameters: vector_id, force
       - Response: scan_id, status

GET    /api/v1/monitor/scan/:scan_id
       - Get scan results
       - Response: results, recommendations

POST   /api/v1/monitor/scan/batch
       - Trigger multiple vector scans
       - Parameters: vector_ids[]
       - Response: batch_id, status

GET    /api/v1/monitor/history
       - Get historical scan data
       - Parameters: start_date, end_date, vectors[]
       - Response: historical_data

POST   /api/v1/monitor/alerts/subscribe
       - Subscribe to alerts for specific vectors
       - Parameters: vector_ids[], channels[]
       - Response: subscription_id, status

GET    /api/v1/monitor/alerts/notifications
       - Get alert notifications
       - Parameters: since, vectors[]
       - Response: notifications
```

#### Vector Endpoints

```
GET    /api/v1/vectors/:vector_id
       - Get vector definition and configuration
       - Response: vector_spec, config_options

POST   /api/v1/vectors/:vector_id/config
       - Update vector configuration
       - Parameters: config_options
       - Response: updated_config

POST   /api/v1/vectors/:vector_id/status
       - Get vector status
       - Response: status, metrics, last_run
```

#### Data Broker Endpoints

```
POST   /api/v1/data-broker/remove
       - Submit removal request
       - Parameters: broker_ids[], user_data
       - Response: tracking_id, status

GET    /api/v1/data-broker/:tracking_id
       - Get removal request status
       - Response: status, broker_info, progress

POST   /api/v1/data-broker/:tracking_id/verify
       - Verify removal completion
       - Parameters: verification_method
       - Response: verification_result, certificate

GET    /api/v1/data-broker/list
       - List all brokers
       - Parameters: category, limit, offset
       - Response: broker_list

POST   /api/v1/data-broker/batch/remove
       - Batch removal request
       - Parameters: broker_ids[], user_data
       - Response: batch_id, status
```

#### Security Posture Endpoints

```
GET    /api/v1/security/posture
       - Get current security posture
       - Response: score, vectors, overall_status

POST   /api/v1/security/posture/recommend
       - Get security recommendations
       - Parameters: vectors[], priority
       - Response: recommendations, steps

GET    /api/v1/security/history
       - Get security posture history
       - Parameters: start_date, end_date
       - Response: posture_timeline

POST   /api/v1/security/risk-assess
       - Perform risk assessment
       - Parameters: vectors[], severity
       - Response: risk_score, factors
```

#### User Management Endpoints

```
POST   /api/v1/users/auth/login
       - User authentication
       - Parameters: credentials
       - Response: token, user_data

POST   /api/v1/users/auth/register
       - User registration
       - Parameters: registration_data
       - Response: user_id, token

GET    /api/v1/users/profile
       - Get user profile
       - Parameters: user_id
       - Response: profile, settings

PUT    /api/v1/users/profile
       - Update user profile
       - Parameters: profile_data
       - Response: updated_profile

DELETE /api/v1/users/:user_id
       - Delete user account
       - Parameters: user_id
       - Response: deletion_status
```

#### Dashboard Endpoints

```
GET    /api/v1/dashboard/summary
       - Get dashboard summary
       - Response: overview, trends, alerts

GET    /api/v1/dashboard/vector-status
       - Get vector status summary
       - Response: vector_statuses, scores

GET    /api/v1/dashboard/visuals
       - Get dashboard visualizations
       - Parameters: type, time_range
       - Response: chart_data, metrics

POST   /api/v1/dashboard/export
       - Export dashboard data
       - Parameters: format, date_range
       - Response: export_file_url
```

```
wss://api.agape-sovereign.com/ws/v1

Subscribe:
POST /ws/subscribe
  - Parameters: subscription_id, vector_ids[], channels[]

Message Types:

Publish:
POST /ws/publish
  - Parameters: message_type, payload
  - Response: confirmation

## Backend Infrastructure

### Technology Stack

**Frontend**
- React 18+ with TypeScript
- Next.js 14 (App Router)
- Tailwind CSS
- Redux Toolkit for state management
- React Query for data fetching

**Backend**
- Node.js 20+ with TypeScript
- Express.js or FastAPI
- PostgreSQL 15+ for relational data
- Redis for caching and sessions
- MongoDB for unstructured data

**Infrastructure**
- Docker containers
- Kubernetes orchestration
- AWS/GCP/Azure cloud deployment
- Cloudflare CDN for static assets
- PostgreSQL replica for read scaling

### Database Schema

```sql
-- Users table
CREATE TABLE users (
  user_id UUID PRIMARY KEY,
  email VARCHAR(255) UNIQUE,
  password_hash VARCHAR(255),
  api_key VARCHAR(255),
  created_at TIMESTAMP,
  updated_at TIMESTAMP
);

-- Monitoring sessions
CREATE TABLE monitoring_sessions (
  session_id UUID PRIMARY KEY,
  user_id UUID REFERENCES users(user_id),
  vectors JSONB,
  status VARCHAR(50),
  started_at TIMESTAMP,
  completed_at TIMESTAMP,
  results JSONB
);

-- Scan results
CREATE TABLE scan_results (
  result_id UUID PRIMARY KEY,
  session_id UUID REFERENCES monitoring_sessions(session_id),
  vector_id VARCHAR(50),
  status VARCHAR(50),
  score INTEGER,
  findings JSONB,
  recommendations JSONB,
  created_at TIMESTAMP
);

-- Data broker removals
CREATE TABLE removal_requests (
  request_id UUID PRIMARY KEY,
  user_id UUID REFERENCES users(user_id),
  tracking_id VARCHAR(255) UNIQUE,
  broker_id VARCHAR(255),
  status VARCHAR(50),
  created_at TIMESTAMP,
  updated_at TIMESTAMP,
  verification_status VARCHAR(50)
);

-- Alerts
CREATE TABLE alerts (
  alert_id UUID PRIMARY KEY,
  user_id UUID REFERENCES users(user_id),
  vector_id VARCHAR(50),
  severity VARCHAR(50),
  message TEXT,
  created_at TIMESTAMP,
  acknowledged BOOLEAN,
  resolved BOOLEAN
);
```

## Security Implementation

### Authentication & Authorization

1. **API Key Authentication**
   - HMAC-based API key verification
   - Rate limiting per key
   - IP whitelisting option

2. **OAuth 2.0**
   - Client credentials flow
   - Authorization code flow
   - Refresh token rotation

3. **JWT Tokens**
   - Short-lived access tokens
   - Long-lived refresh tokens
   - Token blacklisting

### Data Protection

1. **Encryption**
   - TLS 1.3 for all communications
   - AES-256 for data at rest
   - Key management with HSM

2. **Access Control**
   - Role-based access control (RBAC)
   - Row-level security
   - Audit logging

3. **Input Validation**
   - Schema validation
   - SQL injection prevention
   - XSS prevention
   - Rate limiting

Public endpoints: 100 requests/hour
Authenticated: 1000 requests/hour
Premium: 5000 requests/hour
Enterprise: Unlimited
```

### Throttling Strategy
- Sliding window rate limiting
- Burst allowance (2x normal rate for 1 minute)
- Gradual escalation for exceeding limits
- Graceful degradation

| 6 | `agape-sovereign` | Comprehensive service architecture document | ✅ Created |

---

### **Service Overview:**

**Agape-Sovereign** is designed as:
- 🌐 **Progressive Web App (PWA)** - Works offline, installable on all platforms
- 🚀 **Unlimited Professional Use** - No usage limits for enterprise clients
- 📱 **Multi-Platform** - Web, Mobile, Desktop with seamless sync
- 🔒 **Enterprise Security** - TLS 1.3, AES-256, OAuth 2.0, RBAC
- 📊 **16 Identity Vectors** - Comprehensive breach detection & monitoring
- 🤖 **Automated Remediation** - Self-service removal & security actions
- 💰 **3 Pricing Tiers** - Free ($0), Professional ($29.99/mo), Enterprise (Custom)

---

### **Key Features:**

1. **Real-time Monitoring** - 16 vectors across breach, social, device, financial, medical, biometric data
2. **Automated Data Broker Removal** - Template generation, tracking, verification
3. **Offline-First PWA** - Works without internet, syncs when connected
4. **RESTful API** - Full programmatic access for integrations
5. **WebSocket Alerts** - Real-time breach notifications
6. **Enterprise Compliance** - GDPR, CCPA, HIPAA, SOC 2 ready
7. **Push Notifications** - Critical alerts delivered instantly

[
  {
    "old_string": "",
    "new_string": "import { NextResponse } from 'next/server';\nimport type { NextRequest } from 'next/server';\nimport jwt from 'jose';\n\nconst secret = new TextEncoder().encode(process.env.JWT_SECRET || 'agape-sovereign-secret');\n\nconst protectedRoutes = ['/dashboard', '/breaches', '/settings'];\nconst publicRoutes = ['/login', '/register'];\n\nasync function verifyToken(request: NextRequest) {\n  try {\n    const token = request.cookies.get('auth_token')?.value;\n    if (!token) return null;\n\n    const decoded = await jwt.verify(token, secret);\n    return { userId: decoded.userId, email: decoded.email };\n  } catch {\n    return null;\n  }\n}\n\nexport async function middleware(request: NextRequest) {\n  const { userId } = await verifyToken(request);\n  const { pathname } = request.nextUrl;\n\n  // Check if route should be protected\n  if (protectedRoutes.some(route => pathname.startsWith(route))) {\n    if (!userId) {\n      const loginUrl = NextResponse.redirect(new URL('/login', request.url));\n      loginUrl.cookies.set('auth_token', '', { maxAge: 0, path: '/' });\n      return loginUrl;\n    }\n  }\n\n  // Check if route should be public\n  if (publicRoutes.some(route => pathname.startsWith(route))) {\n    if (userId) {\n      const dashboardUrl = NextResponse.redirect(new URL('/dashboard', request.url));\n      return dashboardUrl;\n    }\n  }\n\n  return NextResponse.next();\n}\n\nexport const config = {\n  matcher: [\n    /*\n     * Match all request paths except:\n     * - _next/static (static files)\n     * - _next/image (image optimization files)\n     * - favicon.ico (favicon file)\n     * - public folder\n     */\n    '/((?!_next/static|_next/image|favicon.ico|public).*)',\n  ],\n};\n",
    "replace_all": false
  }
]

[
  {
    "old_string": "",
    "new_string": "const CACHE_NAME = 'agape-sovereign-v1';\nconst CACHE_URLS = [\n  '/',\n  '/index.html',\n  '/manifest.json',\n  '/icons/icon-192x192.png',\n  '/icons/icon-512x512.png',\n];\n\n// Install event - cache everything\nself.addEventListener('install', (event) => {\n  event.waitUntil(\n    caches.open(CACHE_NAME)\n      .then((cache) => cache.addAll(CACHE_URLS))\n      .then(() => self.skipWaiting())\n  );\n});\n\n// Activate event - clean old caches\nself.addEventListener('activate', (event) => {\n  event.waitUntil(\n    caches.keys()\n      .then((cacheNames) =>\n        Promise.all(\n          cacheNames.filter((name) => name !== CACHE_NAME)\n            .map((name) => caches.delete(name))\n        )\n      )\n      .then(() => self.clients.claim())\n  );\n});\n\n// Fetch event - network first strategy\nself.addEventListener('fetch', (event) => {\n  event.respondWith(\n    fetch(event.request)\n      .then((response) => {\n        // Clone response\n        const responseClone = response.clone();\n        caches.open(CACHE_NAME)\n          .then((cache) => cache.put(event.request, responseClone));\n        return response;\n      })\n      .catch(() => {\n        // Fallback to cache\n        return caches.match(event.request);\n      })\n  );\n});\n\n// Background sync for offline data\nself.addEventListener('sync', (event) => {\n  if (event.tag === 'sync-breach-data') {\n    event.waitUntil(syncBreachData());\n  }\n});\n\nasync function syncBreachData() {\n  // Sync breach data when network is available\n  const clients = await self.clients.matchAll();\n  clients.forEach((client) => {\n    client.postMessage({ type: 'SYNC_COMPLETE' });\n  });\n}\n\n// Push notifications\nself.addEventListener('push', (event) => {\n  const data = event.data?.json();\n  const options = {\n    body: data?.body || 'New breach detected!',\n    icon: '/icons/icon-192x192.png',\n    badge: '/icons/icon-96x96.png',\n    vibrate: [200, 100, 200],\n    data: { next: '00' },\n    tag: data?.tag || 'agape-notification',\n    renotify: true,\n  };\n  event.waitUntil(\n    self.registration.showNotification(data?.title || 'Agape Sovereign', options)\n  );\n});\n\n// Notification click\nself.addEventListener('notificationclick', (event) => {\n  event.notification.close();\n  event.waitUntil(\n    clients.openWindow(event.notification.data.next || '/')\n  );\n});\n",
    "replace_all": false
  }
]

# Agape-Sovereign Core Orchestrator

You are the central orchestrator for the Agape-Sovereign identity monitoring service. You coordinate all 16 identity vectors to provide comprehensive, continuous identity security monitoring.

## Architecture Overview

Agape-Sovereign operates as a Progressive Web App (PWA) with:
- **Multi-platform compatibility** (Web, Mobile, Desktop)
- **Open API** for external integrations
- **Unlimited usage** for professional/enterprise clients
- **Privacy-first design** with local processing where possible

## The 16 Identity Vectors

### Critical Tier (Immediate Priority)
| # | Vector | Priority | Description |
|---|--------|----------|-------------|
| 1 | Email Breach Scanner | 🔴 Critical | Breach detection, metadata exposure |
| 5 | Deep Web Exposure | 🔴 Critical | Pattern-based lookup monitoring |
| 6 | Data Broker Removal | 🔴 Critical | Automated removal template generation |
| 7 | Password Vault Analysis | 🔴 Critical | Weak credential detection |
| 10 | Financial Identity Exposure | 🔴 Critical | Banking/payment data leaks |
| 11 | Medical Data Footprint | 🔴 Critical | Health record exposure |
| 12 | Voice & Biometric Data | 🔴 Critical | Biometric sample detection |
| 15 | Dark Web Monitoring | 🔴 Critical | Dark web credential indexing |

### High Tier (Scheduled Priority)
| # | Vector | Priority | Description |
|---|--------|----------|-------------|
| 2 | Social Media Footprint | 🟡 High | Username reuse, profile scraping |
| 3 | Device File Scan | 🟡 High | Local & cloud file analysis |
| 8 | Location Data Footprint | 🟡 High | GPS history & metadata exposure |
| 9 | Browser & Cookie Tracker | 🟡 High | Third-party tracking detection |
| 13 | IoT & Smart Device Scan | 🟡 High | Connected device security audit |
| 14 | Cloud Storage Exposure | 🟡 High | Google Drive, OneDrive, iCloud analysis |
| 16 | Behavioral Profile Analysis | 🟡 High | Inferred demographic mapping |

### Medium Tier (Ongoing Monitoring)
| # | Vector | Priority | Description |
|---|--------|----------|-------------|
| 4 | Mobile Security Layer | 🟢 Medium | Passkey enforcement, 2FA status |

## Core Functions

### 1. Continuous Monitoring
- **Real-time breach detection** across all vectors
- **Scheduled scans** (hourly/daily/weekly based on user preference)
- **Alert system** for critical threshold breaches
- **Trend analysis** over time

### 2. Automated Remediation
- **Template generation** for data broker removal
- **Password reset automation** for compromised accounts
- **2FA enforcement** across all accounts
- **Auto-removal** requests to data brokers

### 3. Privacy Dashboard
- **Visual security posture** scoring
- **Vector-by-vector status** indicators
- **Actionable recommendations** with step-by-step guides
- **Historical trend** charts and analytics

### 4. API Integration
- **RESTful API** for external integrations
- **WebSocket** for real-time alerts
- **OAuth 2.0** for secure authentication
- **API keys** for programmatic access

## Usage Patterns

### Professional/Enterprise Use
- Unlimited monitoring cycles
- Multi-user support with role-based access
- Custom vector prioritization
- API access for integrations
- SLA-backed response times

### Personal Use
- Free tier with 10 monitoring cycles/month
- Basic vector coverage
- Standard alert notifications
- Community support

### Premium/Enterprise
- Unlimited monitoring cycles
- All 16 vectors with deep analysis
- Priority support and response
- Custom integrations
- On-premise deployment option

## Response Format

Structure all responses as:

```
# Agape-Sovereign Identity Monitor

## Current Security Posture
[Overall score and risk assessment]

## Vector Status Summary
[Quick status for all 16 vectors]

## Critical Alerts
[List of immediate action items]

## Recommended Actions
[Prioritized action plan]

## Technical Details
[API endpoints, tools, services used]
```

## Important Considerations

1. **Privacy First**: Process sensitive data locally where possible
2. **Compliance**: GDPR, CCPA, HIPAA compliance where applicable
3. **Transparency**: Clearly show what data is being analyzed
4. **User Control**: Allow users to disable any vector at any time
5. **Continuous Improvement**: Regular updates to vector detection methods

# Agape-Sovereign PWA Architecture

You are the PWA architecture specialist for Agape-Sovereign. You design and implement Progressive Web App features for multi-platform identity monitoring.

## PWA Architecture Overview

Agape-Sovereign operates as a Progressive Web App (PWA) with offline-first capabilities, cross-platform compatibility, and seamless user experience.

## Core PWA Components

### 1. Service Worker Architecture

**Offline-First Design**
- Cache critical monitoring data locally
- Queue monitoring tasks for offline execution
- Resume monitoring when connection restored
- Local storage for sensitive data (encrypted)

**Service Worker Endpoints**
```
/api/v1/monitoring/queue
/api/v1/monitoring/resume
/api/v1/monitoring/notify
/api/v1/cache/identity-data
```

### 2. Multi-Platform Compatibility

**Responsive Design**
- Mobile-first CSS architecture
- Touch-optimized controls
- Tablet and desktop layouts
- Accessibility (WCAG 2.1 AA compliant)

**Platform-Specific Features**
- **iOS**: Safari PWA, App Store listing
- **Android**: Chrome PWA, Play Store listing
- **Desktop**: Electron wrapper option
- **Cross-platform**: WebAssembly for heavy processing

### 3. Security Architecture

**Client-Side Security**
- Web Crypto API for encryption
- Subtle encryption for sensitive data
- Secure storage (IndexedDB, localStorage with encryption)
- Biometric authentication (FaceID, TouchID)

**Data Protection**
- AES-256 encryption for stored data
- Key derivation using PBKDF2
- Secure deletion protocols
- Encrypted backups

### 4. Performance Optimization

**Caching Strategy**
- Service Worker caching (Cache API)
- IndexedDB for large datasets
- CDN for static assets
- Resource pre-fetching

**Monitoring Optimization**
- Background tasks (Service Worker scheduling)
- Batch processing for multiple vectors
- Smart throttling for bandwidth constraints
- Caching breach databases locally

### 5. User Experience

**Push Notifications**
- Critical breach alerts
- Scheduled scan reminders
- Removal request status updates
- Priority notification levels

**Offline Experience**
- View cached security posture
- Queue actions for later execution
- Sync when connection restored
- Local data access

### 6. API Integration

**RESTful API Endpoints**
```
POST /api/v1/monitor/start  - Start monitoring session
GET  /api/v1/monitor/status - Get monitoring status
POST /api/v1/monitor/scan  - Trigger specific vector scan
GET  /api/v1/monitor/results - Get scan results
POST /api/v1/monitor/queue - Queue scan for later
GET  /api/v1/monitor/history - Get historical data
POST /api/v1/monitor/alerts - Subscribe to alerts
GET  /api/v1/monitor/health  - Health check
```

**WebSocket Integration**
- Real-time alert notifications
- Push notification delivery
- Live security posture updates
- Bidirectional communication

### 7. Deployment Architecture

**Production Stacks**
- **Frontend**: React/Next.js with PWA manifest
- **Backend**: Node.js/Express or Python/FastAPI
- **Database**: PostgreSQL for structured data, Redis for caching
- **Storage**: S3-compatible object storage
- **CDN**: Cloudflare or similar for global distribution

**Edge Deployment**
- Serverless functions (AWS Lambda, Cloudflare Workers)
- Edge caching for static assets
- Global CDN for low latency
- Multi-region deployment

### 8. Testing & Validation

**PWA Testing Checklist**
- [ ] Installable from browser
- [ ] Works offline
- [ ] Push notifications enabled
- [ ] Responsive on all devices
- [ ] App Store/Play Store ready
- [ ] Accessibility compliant
- [ ] Security hardened

**Cross-Platform Testing**
- iOS Safari (latest 2 versions)
- Android Chrome (latest 2 versions)
- Desktop Chrome, Firefox, Safari
- Tablet devices
- Low-bandwidth scenarios

## Usage Examples

### Starting a Monitoring Session
```
User clicks "Start Monitoring"
→ Service Worker queues scan task
→ Browser caches sensitive data
→ Scan executes in background
→ Results push to user via WebSocket
→ Alert notification sent if critical breach
```

### Offline Monitoring
```
User has no internet connection
→ PWA uses cached breach databases
→ Scan completes locally
→ Results stored in encrypted local storage
→ Syncs when connection restored
→ Historical data accessible offline
```

### Push Notification Delivery
```
Critical breach detected
→ WebSocket sends alert to service worker
→ Service Worker triggers push notification
→ User receives immediate alert
→ Detailed report available in app
```

## Security Best Practices

1. **Encrypt all sensitive data** before storage
2. **Use HTTPS** exclusively
3. **Implement CSRF protection**
4. **Rate limit API endpoints**
5. **Validate all user inputs**
6. **Secure service worker** (subtle encryption)
7. **Implement proper CORS policies**
8. **Regular security audits**

## Performance Metrics

- **Time to Interactive**: < 3 seconds
- **First Contentful Paint**: < 1.5 seconds
- **Scan completion time**: < 30 seconds per vector
- **Push notification delivery**: < 5 seconds
- **Offline data sync**: < 10 seconds
- **App size**: < 5MB (excluding service worker)

## Continuous Improvement

- Regular PWA updates (monthly)
- Security patching (weekly)
- Performance optimization (quarterly)
- User feedback integration (continuous)
- A/B testing for UX improvements

# Agape Sovereign AI

> **Digital Identity Federated Footprint (DIFF) Intelligence Platform** — Scan, analyze, and reclaim control over your digital identity across 16 attack vectors with zero-knowledge encryption and AI-powered sovereignty.

**Live:** [sovereign.nyc](https://sovereign.nyc) | **Status:** Production | **Firebase Project:** `agape-sovereign`

---

## What is Agape Sovereign AI?

Agape Sovereign AI is a privacy-first platform for **Digital Identity Federated Footprint (DIFF)** intelligence. It empowers users to scan, analyze, and reclaim control over their digital identity across 16 identity vectors — from email breaches to biometric exposure — using AI analysis and compliance-grade encrypted reporting.

Built entirely on **Firebase free-tier infrastructure** + **Google AI services** (Gemini API), with no paid cloud dependencies.

---

## Core Features

| Feature | Description |
|---------|-------------|
| **DIFF Scanner** | 16-vector digital identity analysis (email, social, device, financial, dark web, biometric, IoT, cloud, etc.) |
| **Sovereign Score** | Lighthouse-style 0–100 privacy health metric (KNOXED / NUKED classification) |
| **Architect AI** | Conversational AI privacy assistant powered by Gemini |
| **Passkey Auth** | FIDO2 WebAuthn device-bound authentication (no passwords) |
| **Google OAuth 2.0** | Primary authentication with Apple ID federation |
| **Zero-Knowledge Encryption** | AES-256-GCM client-side encryption — your data never leaves your device unencrypted |
| **Compliance Reports** | SHA256-signed PDF reports with 2-year retention (ECRA 2026, GDPR, CCPA) |
| **Admin Portal** | Infrastructure monitoring, audit trail querying, WebAuthn logs |

## Quick Start

### Prerequisites

1. **Node.js 20+** — [Download](https://nodejs.org/)
2. **Firebase CLI** — `npm install -g firebase-tools`
3. **Google Cloud Project** with Firebase enabled
4. **Google OAuth credentials** configured in Firebase Console

### Installation

```bash
# Clone the repository
git clone https://github.com/izrl613/agape-sovereign.git
cd agape-sovereign

# Install root dependencies
npm install

# Install Cloud Functions dependencies
cd functions && npm install && cd ..

# Copy environment configuration
cp .env.example .env
# Edit .env with your Firebase config
```

### Firebase Configuration

Create `.env` with your Firebase project config:

```env
# Firebase Config (from Firebase Console > Project Settings > General > Your apps)
VITE_FIREBASE_API_KEY=your-api-key
VITE_FIREBASE_AUTH_DOMAIN=sovereign.nyc
VITE_FIREBASE_PROJECT_ID=agape-sovereign
VITE_FIREBASE_STORAGE_BUCKET=agape-sovereign.firebasestorage.app
VITE_FIREBASE_MESSAGING_SENDER_ID=your-sender-id
VITE_FIREBASE_APP_ID=your-app-id
VITE_FIREBASE_MEASUREMENT_ID=your-measurement-id

# Functions secrets (set via Firebase CLI)
# firebase functions:secrets:set PASSKEY_COOKIE_SECRET
```

### Development

```bash
# Start Vite dev server (frontend)
npm run dev

# In another terminal: start Firebase emulators
firebase emulators:start

# Or start only functions emulator
firebase emulators:start --only functions,firestore,auth
```

**Frontend:** `http://localhost:5173`  
**Functions Emulator:** `http://localhost:5001`  
**Firestore Emulator:** `http://localhost:8080`  
**Auth Emulator:** `http://localhost:9099`

### Production Build & Deploy

```bash
# Build frontend
npm run build

# Build functions
npm run build:functions

# Deploy to Firebase
firebase deploy

# Deploy only specific services
firebase deploy --only hosting
firebase deploy --only functions
firebase deploy --only firestore:rules
```

---

## Available Scripts

```bash
# Root level
npm run dev              # Start Vite dev server
npm run build            # Build frontend for production
npm run build:all        # Build frontend + functions
npm run build:frontend   # Build frontend only
npm run build:functions  # Build Cloud Functions
npm run deploy           # Full Firebase deploy
npm run lint             # Lint functions
npm run typecheck        # TypeScript check (root + functions)
npm run logs             # View Cloud Functions logs
```

## Authentication Flow

### Google OAuth 2.0 (Primary)
1. User clicks "Sign in with Google"
2. Firebase Auth handles OAuth flow via `sovereign.nyc/__/auth/handler`
3. On success, user document created/updated in Firestore
4. **Gatekeeper** computes SHA-256 sovereign hash (session identifier)
5. User redirected to Dashboard

### Universal Passkey (WebAuthn/FIDO2)
1. After Google sign-in, `PasskeySetupPrompt` offers passkey binding
2. User registers device-bound passkey via WebAuthn
3. Credential stored in Firestore (`/users/{uid}/passkeyCredentials`)
4. Subsequent logins use passkey (no password, no Google prompt)

**Passkey Endpoints (Cloud Functions):**
- `POST /api/auth/register-options` — Get registration challenge
- `POST /api/auth/verify-registration` — Verify & store credential, return custom token
- `POST /api/auth/login-options` — Get authentication challenge
- `POST /api/auth/verify-login` — Verify assertion, return custom token

---

## Firestore Collections

| Collection | Description |
|-----------|-------------|
| `/users/{userId}` | User profiles, passkey binding status, sovereign score |
| `/diff_scans/{scanId}` | DIFF scan results, 16-vector details |
| `/diff_reports/{reportId}` | Generated PDF report metadata |
| `/audit_logs/{logId}` | Immutable security event log |

---

## Security Model

- **Zero-Knowledge Architecture**: User data encrypted client-side (AES-256-GCM) before upload
- **Admin-Blind**: Even project admins cannot decrypt user data
- **Passkey-First**: FIDO2 WebAuthn device-bound credentials (phishing-resistant)
- **Sovereign Hash**: SHA-256 identity hash computed per session (Gatekeeper) — raw UID/email never stored beyond scope
- **Immutable Audit Trail**: All security events logged to `/audit_logs`
- **Firebase App Check**: Request attestation prevents abuse

---

## Compliance

- **ECRA 2026** — European Cybersecurity Resilience Act
- **GDPR** — Data minimization, consent, right to deletion
- **CCPA** — California Privacy Rights Act

PDF reports include SHA256 fingerprint, Cloud Audit ID, and 2-year retention in Firebase Storage.

---

## Firebase Quotas (Free Tier)

| Service | Daily Limit | Estimated Capacity |
|---------|------------|-------------------|
| Firestore Reads | 50,000 | ~1,000 DIFF scans |
| Firestore Writes | 20,000 | 16 vectors × 20 scans |
| Firebase Storage | 5 GB total | ~2,500 PDF reports/month |
| Cloud Functions | 2M invocations/month | ~66K scans/month |
| Gemini API | 60 RPM, 1M TPM | ~4,000 chat sessions/day |

---

## Cloud Functions

| Function | Trigger | Purpose |
|----------|---------|---------|
| `authApi` | HTTP (Hosting rewrite `/api/auth/**`) | WebAuthn registration & authentication |
| `initiateDIFFScan` | HTTP call | Start a new 16-vector scan |
| `generateDIFFReport` | Firestore write | Generate PDF after scan completion |
| `cleanupOldReports` | Scheduled (daily) | Delete reports older than 2 years |
| `healthCheck` | HTTP call | Infrastructure monitoring endpoint |

generator client {
  provider = "prisma-client-js"
}

datasource db {
  provider = "postgresql"
  url      = env("DATABASE_URL")
}

// User model
model User {
  id            String    @id @default(cuid())
  email         String    @unique
  username      String
  passwordHash  String
  createdAt     DateTime  @default(now())
  updatedAt     DateTime  @updatedAt
  breaches      Breach[]  @relation("UserBreaches")
  notifications Notification[]
}

// Breach model
model Breach {
  id          String   @id @default(cuid())
  email       String
  username    String?
  password    String?
  hash        String?
  createdAt   DateTime @default(now())
  updatedAt   DateTime @updatedAt
  userId      String?
  user        User?    @relation("UserBreaches", fields: [userId], references: [id])
}

// Notification model
model Notification {
  id        String   @id @default(cuid())
  userId    String
  user      User     @relation(fields: [userId], references: [id], onDelete: Cascade)
  title     String
  message   String
  type      String   // info, warning, danger, success
  isRead    Boolean  @default(false)
  createdAt DateTime @default(now())
}

```

import os
import json
import shutil
from datetime import datetime

# Create the export script
export_script = '''
#!/usr/bin/env python3
"""
Agape Sovereign - Complete Export to GitHub, GCP, and Firebase
This script exports all coded files and sets up deployment
"""

import os
import json
import subprocess
from pathlib import Path

# Configuration
PROJECT_DIR = "izrl613/agape0sovereign"
GITHUB_REPO = "izrl613/agape-sovereign"
FIREBASE_PROJECT = "agape-sovereign"

def print_section(title):
    print(f"\\n{'='*60}")
    print(f"  {title}")
    print(f"{'='*60}\\n")

def list_all_files():
    """List all files in the project"""
    print_section("📁 Listing All Project Files")
    files = []
    for root, dirs, filenames in os.walk(PROJECT_DIR):
        for filename in filenames:
            filepath = os.path.join(root, filename)
            files.append(filepath)
    files.sort()
    for f in files:
        print(f"  {f}")
    return files

def create_gitignore():
    """Ensure .gitignore is properly configured"""
    gitignore = os.path.join(PROJECT_DIR, ".gitignore")
    if not os.path.exists(gitignore):
        with open(gitignore, "w") as f:
            f.write("""# Dependencies
node_modules/
.pnpm-store/

# Environment files
.env
.env.local
.env.production
.env.development
*.env

# Build outputs
dist/
.next/
build/

# IDE
.idea/
.vscode/
*.swp
*.swo

# OS files
.DS_Store
Thumbs.db

# Logs
*.log
npm-debug.log*
yarn-debug.log*
yarn-error.log*

# Test outputs
coverage/
.nyc_output/

# Temporary files
tmp/
temp/
.cache/
""")
    print("✅ .gitignore configured")

def create_package_json():
    """Ensure package.json exists with proper scripts"""
    package_json = os.path.join(PROJECT_DIR, "package.json")
    if not os.path.exists(package_json):
        with open(package_json, "w") as f:
            json.dump({
                "name": "agape-sovereign",
                "version": "1.0.0",
                "description": "Agape Sovereign - Real PWA Implementation with Breach Detection",
                "private": True,
                "scripts": {
                    "dev": "next dev",
                    "build": "next build",
                    "start": "next start",
                    "lint": "next lint",
                    "test": "cypress run",
                    "test:e2e": "cypress run",
                    "db:generate": "prisma migrate dev",
                    "db:deploy": "prisma migrate deploy",
                    "db:push": "prisma db push",
                    "db:studio": "prisma studio",
                    "db:seed": "prisma db seed",
                    "deploy": "firebase deploy",
                    "deploy:frontend": "firebase deploy --only hosting",
                    "deploy:functions": "firebase deploy --only functions",
                    "deploy:all": "firebase deploy"
                },
                "dependencies": {
                    "@prisma/client": "^5.21.0",
                    "@tanstack/react-query": "^5.62.14",
                    "@vercel/analytics": "^1.3.2",
                    "axios": "^1.7.7",

                    "prisma": "^5.21.0",
                    "tailwindcss": "^3.4.15",
                    "typescript": "^5.7.2"
                }
            }, indent=2)
    print("✅ package.json configured")

def create_next_config():
    """Create Next.js configuration"""
    config = os.path.join(PROJECT_DIR, "next.config.js")
    with open(config, "w") as f:
        f.write("""/** @type {import('next').NextConfig} */
const nextConfig = {
  reactStrictMode: true,
  transpilePackages: ['@agape-sovereign/*'],
  images: {
    unoptimized: true,
  },
  output: 'standalone',
  env: {
    NEXT_PUBLIC_APP_NAME: 'agape-sovereign',
  },
};

module.exports = nextConfig;
""")
    print("✅ next.config.js configured")

def create_tailwind_config():
    """Create Tailwind CSS configuration"""
    config = os.path.join(PROJECT_DIR, "tailwind.config.js")
    with open(config, "w") as f:
        f.write("""/** @type {import('tailwindcss').Config} */
module.exports = {
  content: [
    './src/pages/**/*.{js,ts,jsx,tsx,mdx}',
    './src/components/**/*.{js,ts,jsx,tsx,mdx}',
    './src/app/**/*.{js,ts,jsx,tsx,mdx}',
  ],
  theme: {
    extend: {
      colors: {
        primary: {
          50: '#eff6ff',
          100: '#dbeafe',

def create_tsconfig():
    """Create TypeScript configuration"""
    config = os.path.join(PROJECT_DIR, "tsconfig.json")
    with open(config, "w") as f:
        f.write("""{
  "compilerOptions": {
    "target": "ES2022",
    "lib": ["dom", "dom.iterable", "ES2022"],
    "allowJs": true,
    "skipLibCheck": true,
    "strict": true,
    "noEmit": true,
    "esModuleInterop": true,
    "module": "ESNext",
    "moduleResolution": "bundler",
    "resolveJsonModule": true,
    "isolatedModules": true,
    "jsx": "preserve",
    "incremental": true,
    "plugins": [
      {
        "name": "next"
      }
    ],
    "paths": {
      "@/*": ["./src/*"]
    },
    "types": ["next", "node"],
    "noUncheckedIndexedAccess": true,
    "strictNullChecks": true
  },
  "include": ["next-env.d.ts", "**/*.ts", "**/*.tsx", ".next/types/**/*.ts"],
  "exclude": ["node_modules"]
}
""")
    print("✅ tsconfig.json configured")

def create_prisma_schema():
    """Create Prisma schema"""
    schema = os.path.join(PROJECT_DIR, "prisma/schema.prisma")
    with open(schema, "w") as f:
        f.write("""// This is your Prisma schema file,
// learn more about it in the docs: https://pris.ly/d/prisma-schema

generator client {
  provider = "prisma-client-js"
}

datasource db {
  provider = "postgresql"
  url      = env("DATABASE_URL")
}

model User {
  id            String    @id @default(cuid())
  email         String    @unique
  username      String
  passwordHash  String
  createdAt     DateTime  @default(now())
  updatedAt     DateTime  @updatedAt
  breaches      Breach[]  @relation("UserBreaches")
  notifications Notification[]
}

model Breach {
  id          String   @id @default(cuid())
  email       String
  username    String?
  password    String?
  hash        String?
  createdAt   DateTime @default(now())
  updatedAt   DateTime @updatedAt
  userId      String?
  user        User?    @relation("UserBreaches", fields: [userId], references: [id])
}

model Notification {
  id        String   @id @default(cuid())
  userId    String
  user      User     @relation(fields: [userId], references: [id], onDelete: Cascade)
  title     String
  message   String
  type      String   // info, warning, danger, success
  isRead    Boolean  @default(false)
  createdAt DateTime @default(now())
}
""")
    print("✅ prisma/schema.prisma configured")

volumes:
  postgres_data:
""")

def create_firebase_config():
    """Create Firebase configuration files"""
    firebase_config = os.path.join(PROJECT_DIR, "firebase.json")
    with open(firebase_config, "w") as f:
        f.write("""{
  "hosting": {
    "public": "public",
    "ignore": [
      "firebase.json",
      "**/.*",
      "**/node_modules/**"
    ],
    "rewrites": [
      {
        "source": "**",
        "destination": "/index.html"
      }
    ]
  },
  "firestore": {
    "rules": "firestore.rules",
    "indexes": "firestore.indexes.json"
  },
  "storage": {
    "rules": "storage.rules"
  },
  "functions": {
    "source": "functions",
    "predeploy": [
      "npm --prefix \"$RESOURCE_DIR\" run build:functions"
    ]
  }
}
""")
    
    firestore_rules = os.path.join(PROJECT_DIR, "firestore.rules")
    with open(firestore_rules, "w") as f:
        f.write("""rules_version = '2';
service cloud.firestore {
  match /databases/{database}/documents {
    // Users - only authenticated users can access
    match /users/{userId} {
      allow read: if request.auth != null && request.auth.uid == userId;
      allow create: if request.auth != null && request.auth.uid == userId;
      allow update, delete: if request.auth != null && request.auth.uid == userId;
    }

    // Breaches - authenticated users can read their own data
    match /breaches/{breachId} {
      allow read: if request.auth != null && 
                    (resource.data.email == request.auth.uid || resource.data.email == request.auth.token.email);
      allow create: if request.auth != null && request.auth.uid == request.auth.token.email;
      allow update, delete: if request.auth != null && request.auth.uid == request.auth.token.email;
    }

    // Notifications - users can manage their own notifications
    match /notifications/{notificationId} {
      allow read: if request.auth != null && resource.data.userId == request.auth.uid;
      allow create, update, delete: if request.auth != null && resource.data.userId == request.auth.uid;
    }

    // Audit logs - all authenticated users can read
    match /audit_logs/{logId} {
      allow read: if request.auth != null;
      allow create: if request.auth != null;
    }
  }
}
""")
    
    firestore_indexes = os.path.join(PROJECT_DIR, "firestore.indexes.json")
    with open(firestore_indexes, "w") as f:
        f.write("""{
  "indexes": [
    {
      "collectionGroup": "users",
      "queryScope": "COLLECTION",
      "fields": [
        {
          "fieldPath": "createdAt",
          "order": "ASCENDING",
          "nullsFirst": false
        }
      ]
    },
    {
      "collectionGroup": "breaches",
      "queryScope": "COLLECTION",
      "fields": [
        {
          "fieldPath": "email",
          "order": "ASCENDING",
          "nullsFirst": false
        },
        {
          "fieldPath": "createdAt",
          "order": "ASCENDING",
          "nullsFirst": false
        }
      ]
    }
  ]
}
""")
    
    storage_rules = os.path.join(PROJECT_DIR, "storage.rules")
    with open(storage_rules, "w") as f:
        f.write("""rules_version = '2';
service firebase.storage {
  match /b/{bucket}/o {
    // Allow authenticated users to upload reports
    match /reports/{userId}/{reportId} {
      allow read: if request.auth != null && request.auth.uid == userId;
      allow write: if request.auth != null && request.auth.uid == userId;
    }
    
    // Allow all authenticated users to access public assets
    match /{allPaths=**} {
      allow read: if request.auth != null;
    }
  }
}
""")
    print("✅ Firebase configuration files created")

def create_service_worker():
    """Create Service Worker for PWA"""
    sw = os.path.join(PROJECT_DIR, "public/sw.js")
    with open(sw, "w") as f:
        f.write("""// Service Worker for Agape Sovereign PWA
const CACHE_NAME = 'agape-sovereign-v1';
const CACHE_FILES = [
  '/',
  '/index.html',
  '/manifest.json',
  '/favicon.ico'
];

// Install event - cache files
self.addEventListener('install', (event) => {
  event.waitUntil(
    caches.open(CACHE_NAME)
      .then((cache) => cache.addAll(CACHE_FILES))
      .then(() => self.skipWaiting())
  );
});

// Activate event - clean old caches
self.addEventListener('activate', (event) => {
  event.waitUntil(
    caches.keys().then((cacheNames) => {
      return Promise.all(
        cacheNames
          .filter((name) => name !== CACHE_NAME)
          .map((name) => caches.delete(name))
      );
    }).then(() => self.clients.claim())
  );
});

// Fetch event - cache-first strategy
self.addEventListener('fetch', (event) => {
  event.respondWith(
    caches.match(event.request)
      .then((response) => {
        // Return cached response if found
        return response || fetch(event.request);
      })
      .catch(() => {
        // Fallback to network if cache miss
        return fetch(event.request);
      })
  );
});
""")
    print("✅ Service Worker configured")

def export_to_github():
    """Export files to GitHub"""
    print_section("📤 Exporting to GitHub")
    
    # List all files
    files = list_all_files()
    print(f"Found {len(files)} files to export")
    
    # Create .gitignore if needed
    create_gitignore()
    
    # Create package.json if needed
    create_package_json()
    
    # Create Next.js configuration
    create_next_config()
    create_tailwind_config()
    create_tsconfig()
    create_prisma_schema()
    create_env_example()
    create_dockerfiles()
    create_readme()
    create_firebase_config()
    create_app_json()
    create_postcss_config()
    create_service_worker()
    create_manifest()
    
    # Check if .git directory exists
    git_dir = os.path.join(PROJECT_DIR, ".git")
    if not os.path.exists(git_dir):
        print("⚠️  No .git directory found. Initialize git repository:")
        print(f"  cd {PROJECT_DIR} && git init")
        print(f"  git add .")
        print(f"  git commit -m \"Initial commit - Agape Sovereign export\"")
        print(f"  git remote add origin https://github.com/izrl613/agape-sovereign.git")
        print(f"  git push -u origin main")
    else:
        print("✅ Git repository already initialized")
    
    return files

def setup_gcp_firebase():
    """Setup GCP and Firebase deployment"""
    print_section("🚀 Setting up GCP/Firebase Deployment")
    
    # Check if Firebase CLI is installed
    if not shutil.which("firebase"):
        print("⚠️  Firebase CLI not found. Install with:")
        print("  npm install -g firebase-tools")
    
    # Create deployment script
    deploy_script = os.path.join(PROJECT_DIR, "deploy.sh")
    with open(deploy_script, "w") as f:
        f.write("""#!/bin/bash
# Agape Sovereign Deployment Script

set -e

echo "🚀 Starting Agape Sovereign deployment..."

# Check if Firebase CLI is installed
if ! command -v firebase &> /dev/null; then
    echo "Installing Firebase CLI..."
    npm install -g firebase-tools
fi

# Login to Firebase
echo "Logging into Firebase..."
firebase login

# Initialize Firebase project if needed
echo "Checking Firebase project..."
firebase projects:list

# Deploy all services
echo "Deploying all services..."
npm run deploy

# Deploy only frontend
echo "Deploying frontend..."
npm run deploy:frontend

# Deploy only functions
echo "Deploying functions..."
npm run deploy:functions

# Deploy only database rules
echo "Deploying database rules..."
npm run deploy:rules

echo "✅ Deployment complete!"
""")
    os.chmod(deploy_script, 0o755)
    print("✅ deploy.sh created")
    
    # Create Firebase configuration file
    firebase_config = os.path.join(PROJECT_DIR, "firebase.config.js")
    with open(firebase_config, "w") as f:
        f.write("""// Firebase Configuration
// Replace these values with your actual Firebase project credentials
const firebaseConfig = {

  authDomain: process.env.NEXT_PUBLIC_FIREBASE_AUTH_DOMAIN,
